846. JONATHAN BATTY was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 1 cast-iron plate, value 5s.; 1 door-scraper, value 2s.; 1 dung-fork, value 2s.; 1 pewter plate, value 1s., and 1 brass cock, value 1s., the goods of Thomas Heapley.
THOMAS HEAPLEY. I live in St. John's-wood-road. About nine o'clock in the morning of the 3d of March, I was in the first floor of my house - the child came to say two boys had got over the wall - I ran down stairs with my son and desired him to get over the wall and see what they were about - he got over and called me - I jumped over and saw the prisoner and another, who had got these things, which had been taken from the back of my garden, into the field - I had seen them safe forty-eight hours before, but they were not on the spot where I found them twenty-four hours before - the prisoner fell on his knees, and hoped I would forgive him - he said it was the first time he ever was found taking any thing.
JOHN HEAPLEY. I saw the prisoner and another boy breaking up a cast-iron gutter - I gave the alarm, and went down and found the property over the wall - the prisoner and the other were carrying it - I heard him beg forgiveness.
ROBERT WILLIAMS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner. GUILTY. Aged 15.
Recommended to Mercy. - Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18260406-271]
847. JOHN BARRY and WILLIAM JONES were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, 2 pairs of stockings, value 4s., the goods of John Sansom.
JOHN SANSOM. I live in Brick-lane, Whitechapel, and am a hosier. On the 15th of March the prisoner Jones came to my shop, with another man, and while they were looking at some ribbed worsted stockings, Barry came in; they did not seem to know one another. Barry asked to look at some caps, gloves, and stockings, which wereshown him - he took his hat off, and put it on the counter to try a cap on, I believe - my wife was serving him; Jones and the other were looking at a variety of things. I kept my eye closely upon them; I afterwards saw Barry put his hat upon his head - he had bought one pair of lamb's-wool stockings and a cap, and paid for them; the others agreed for a pair of stockings, paid 6d. on them, and left them - Mr. Davies came in, and told Barry, who was going away, to take off his hat, and two pairs of these stockings were found in it - he first said they must have got in without his knowledge, and then he said one pair was his own, but I knew they were mine; he had what he had bought in his hand. Jones and the other were very near him.
SIMON DAVIDS. I live near Sansom. The prisoners came to my shop to purchase a waistcoat - they gave me a great deal of trouble, and at last Barry offered me money which I would not take; they went away. The other man, who is not here, came in afterwards, and agreed for one - he told me to put it away for him. After they were gone I missed a new waistcoat - I suspected Barry, as he had taken his hat off two or three times. I went and found him in Sansom's shop.
BARRY'S Defence. I bought them.
BARRY - GUILTY. Aged 26.
Confined Six Months.
JONES - NOT GUILTY.

848. MARY CREED was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 2 gowns, value 8s.; 2 sheets, value 7s., and 2 shifts, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Innell.
MARTHA INNELL. I am the wife of Thomas Innell - he lives at No. 5, Peter's-lane, Cow-cross. On the 18th of March the prisoner came to assist me, and to turn my mangle; she staid two or three hours, and about three hours after she was gone I missed a bundle, containing the articles stated in the indictment.
JOHN REEVE. I am a pawnbroker. I have two gowns and a shift, pawned by the prisoner, on the 28th of March.
JOHN CROSS. I am a pawnbroker. I have two sheets, pawned, I believe, by the prisoner, on the 18th of March.
JOHN LOADSMAN. I am an officer. I took the prisoner; she had one of the shifts on.
GEORGE WADDINGTON. I went to Mrs. Cross', where I found the prisoner in bed - I think her mother has led her into it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 14.

[trialAccount t18260406-273]
849. JOSEPH DUNN and CHARLES DUNN were indicted for stealing, on the 20th of March, 6 yards of bed furniture, value 8s.; 2 shirts, value 24s.; 1 tablecloth, value 8s., and 4 handkerchiefs, value 4s., the goods of Thomas William Charles Edwards.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS WILLIAM CHARLES EDWARDS. I live at Homerton. I employed Ann Hand to wash for me. On Monday, the 20th of March, I had occasion to go into the garden, while the clothes were hanging out, and about a quarter before seven o'clock I went into the garden again, when one of the lines was cut and gone; I had given Hand my bed furniture to wash.
ANN HAND. I was employed on the 20th of March, to wash for this gentleman; there was one line, which contained four half-handkerchiefs, a small window curtain, two of master's linen shirts, the bed furniture, and a large table-cloth. I had seen them all safe about half-past six o'clock, when I went for a pail of water; my master called me about seven, and the line was cut at each end, and the things all gone. The line was fourteen yards long; I can swear to the bed furniture - I had separated it for the purpose of being washed, and it corresponds exactly.
WILLIAM COWLING. I am a constable of Hackney. - On the 25th of March I apprehended the two prisoners - I did not search them, but Attfield did.
WILLIAM ATTFIELD. I was with the constable. I searched Joseph Dunn on the 25th of March, about eleven o'clock in the morning, at Hackney, and found this bed furniture, quite wet, wrapped round his body, under his clothes. I asked how he came by it, and he said it was his own - that he had had it washed, and was going to sell it to his brother, at Clapton, as he was very much in want of money, and if any body else could own it he must suffer for it. Garton (who was with me) found this piece of print, which appears to be the same furniture, in the hat of Charles Dunn; I did not hear what he said about it.
CHARLES DUNN'S Defence. As I was coming home I met a watchman in the field, and coming over the fields I saw something white in a dry ditch; I took it home, and showed it to my landlady. On the Sunday some persons came in, and said I had better take it to the first public-house, nearest to where I found it; I was going the next morning to take it to the White Horse public-house, and while I was there Mr. Attfield took my brother; I went with them, and at Worship-street I pulled off my hat, and he saw what was in it.
JOSEPH DUNN - GUILTY. Aged 18.
CHARLES DUNN - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18260406-274]
850. JAMES PERRIS was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of a certain man, whose name is unknown, from his person.
JAMES HILL. I am a copper-plate printer, and live in Greek-street, Soho. On the 30th of March, about three o'clock in the afternoon, I was in St. Martin's-lane; I had come through the church-yard; I saw the prisoner and two others, following a gentleman very closely - the prisoner took a handkerchief from his pocket, and threw it to one of his companions - another person followed his companion, and he threw it into a bookseller's shop, and then ran into a pawnbroker's - we called out, and the shopman came out - we said he was in the shop, and he went and pulled him out; we were going with him to the watch-house, when the prisoner came up with a stick, and said "That is the b-r; rescue the prisoner, and kill him;" he struck me on the head, which stunned me, and he got away - the gentleman who lost the handkerchief, was gone, and we have never seen him.
WILLIAM PICKET. I work at Mr. Vicker's, in Regent-street. As I was coming down Hemmings-row, on Thursday, the 30th of March, about three o'clock, I heard a cry of Stop thief! the prisoner got off, but the one who had the handkerchief, ran on, and threw it into a bookseller's shop; a man, who pursued him, brought it out again - he ran into Mr. Lorton's, the pawnbroker's, and his nephew pulled him out again - we then went into Green-street, where we met the prisoner with a stick, who said "Rescue the prisoner; and kill him;" the prisoner then got away, but the officer came up, and he was taken afterwards.
WILLIAM YOUNG. I am a police-officer of Bow-street. I took charge of the prisoner, and have had the handkerchief - the owner has not been found.
Prisoner's Defence. On Saturday the 1st of April, the man I work with, came and called for me as he was going to Covent Garden; I had not been out from the Monday before; on Saturday, the 1st of April, I came down to get some milk, and my little sister was with me; they were taking some prisoners to Westminster-hall, and I stood to look at them - an officer, who is not here, told me to stand there, which I did about five minutes - he brought another person and asked if it was me - he said Yes, and they took me into a public-house, and asked me if I knew the boy they had given two months to - I said No.
COURT to JAMES HILL. Q. How soon after you received the blow was the prisoner taken? A. After the other was up at the office.
ANN JOHNSON. I live at No. 3, Union-place, Orchard-street, Westminster. From Tuesday last to Thursday last the prisoner was in my room making a pigeon-house; I am quite certain of it - his mother gave him the wood - he had taken medicine, which caused him to be rather ill; he went to his mother after he left me - he lives at home with his mother - he left me about seven o'clock on Thursday last.
JAMES HILL re-examined. This was on Thursday week, the 30th of March.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Life.

856. TRIPHEMA BARTHOLOMEW was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, at Paddington, 1 sheet, value 4s.; 1 table-cloth, value 2s., and 1 blanket, value 5s., the goods of William Daniels, in a lodging-room, in his dwelling-house, against the statute.
WILLIAM DANIELS. I live in Bray-street, Paddington. The prisoner came to lodge at my house on the 16th of February - the articles stated were let with the room; she remained with me till the 2d of March - I then gave her notice to quit; when she went she took the key of the room-door, and a bonnet belonging to my wife; I got an officer to open the door, and missed the articles.
THOMAS PROBERT. I am a pawnbroker, and live in the Edgware-road. I took a blanket in pledge of the prisoner - I have a sheet and table-cloth - the ticket for the sheet is my writing; I do not know who pawned them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MICHAEL MORRIS. I am an officer. I produce the duplicates of the blanket which I found in the prisoner's room.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18260406-276]
857. JAMES GAYTON was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of March, 19 yards of printed cotton, value 10s., the goods of William Spooner.
WILLIAM SPOONER. I am a linen-draper, and live in Chiswell-street. On the 21st of March I saw this print outside the rail, about one o'clock, and about a quarter before two I was informed it was gone.
THOMAS MENDHAM. I am an assistant to Mr. Lazarus. On Thursday the 21st of March, I was in his shop, which is opposite Mr. Spooner's, and saw the prisoner trying to lift the print from the iron where it hung - he had a companion; he then passed up the street, and in a short time returned and handled it again - I turned round to answer a question, and on turning my head again I saw it was gone - I ran out and pursued the prisoner, who took the right hand turning of Finsbury-square - I called, Stop thief! and he dropped the print from his apron - I took it up, and followed; I lost sight of him, but he was stopped, and I brought him back.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me drop the print? A. I did not actually see you, as I stumbled at the time - I was about fifty yards from you.
JAMES BEDLARE. I am a watchmaker. On Thursday, the 21st of March, between ten and eleven o'clock, I saw the prisoner and another at Spooner's door, and two or three hours afterwards I saw him in custody.
SAMUEL BRIDGES. I am an officer. I took the prisoner a few minutes after two o'clock.
Prisoner's Defence. I have a witness here who saw me at home at the time; one witness said it was Wednesday, and two that it was Thursday.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-277]
858. JANE HANNAH LAYFIED was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of March, 3 yards of toilenette, value 5s., the goods of James Christmas.
JAMES CHRISTMAS. I am a tailor, and live Whitechapel-road. I missed some toilenette on Monday, which I had seen safe on Saturday the 10th of March; the prisoner was afterwards apprehended; I told her she was the last person who should have robbed me; she said she did take it - McGrath shewed me a pawnbroker's shop kept by Howse, where I found it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN VANN. I am an officer. The prosecutor applied to me to take the prisoner - on the 28th of March I went to her house, and took her - as we were going to the watch-house she said she hoped Mr. Christmas would forgive her.
MARY McGRATH. I live in Size-yard, Whitechapel. The prisoner called for me to go to the other end of the town; I was not ready - she told me to come on to Aldgate-church - she came there to me and then left me, and went down the Minories, and came with a bundle, which she said was a black silk gown - she went on to a pawnbroker's and told me to wait; I looked in at the window and saw the toilenet there - she then came out and said she could not get enough for it - she pawned it at the corner of a coach yard in St. Giles' - she came out and said, thank God she was rid of her black silk gown.
JOHN HARRISON. I am an officer. I went to Mr. Christmas and got a piece of toilenette; I then went to a pawnbroker in St. Giles's, and compared it with this - it corresponded exactly - it was pawned at Mr. Howse's, but he could not swear to the person.
COURT to MARY McGRATH. Q. Did you say you went into the shop? A. No; I saw it on the counter - Ishowed the shop to Mr. Christmas - it is opposite the church.
GUILTY. Aged 49.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18260406-278]
885. ELIZABETH PETTY was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of March, 1 saw, value 1s. 6d., and 1 mallet, value 1s., the goods of Henry Scott.
HENRY SCOTT. I am a carpenter, and live in Great Saffron-hill. On the 26th of March I went out about four o'clock, and returned about eight - my room door was open and my tools gone.
THOMAS THOMPSON. About seven o'clock in the evening of Easter Tuesday I was in Golden-lane, and saw the prisoner offering these articles for sale at a broker's shop; I asked whose they were - she said her husband's; I asked if there were any marks on them - she said No, but I found some and took her into custody.(Property produced and sworn to).
Prisoner's Defence. I met a woman in Gray's-inn-lane, she was bleeding, and said she had parted from her husband - she took me to this house - I engaged to sleep there that night - and in the morning, as I came down stairs, my foot struck against these things - I offered them for sale in Golden lane.
GUILTY. Aged 49.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18260406-279]
852. GEORGE WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of March, 7 metal weights, value 30s., the goods of Joseph Hammersley.
JOSEPH HAMMERSLEY. I am a brass turner, and live in Clerkenwell-close. On the night of the 14th of March I lost my weights; and next morning the watchman produced them.
ROBERT PRITCHARD. On Tuesday, the 14th of March, about ten minutes before twelve o'clock at night, I was going down Pear-tree-court, and saw the prisoner loitering about, and knowing there had been a robbery opposite, I went down and asked who he was waiting for - he said I person named Crawley - I had lived there three or four years, and never heard of such a name - I detained him till my son got the watchman - he was taken to the watch-house, where he pulled out the weights - three out of his jacket pocket, and four out of his hat.
ROBERT LOOK. I apprehended a prisoner, who is not here now, about eight or nine o'clock at night; I know he and the prisoner were in company together.
Prisoner. Q. He stated before the Magistate that the clock was striking nine? Q. I did not; I was at the sign of the Duke of Gloucester public-house, and saw the prisoner come in with a man named Caton.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years
[trialAccount t18260406-280]
853. THOMAS WILKINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 1 pair of stays, value 14s., the goods of Frederick Wright Cook.
ELIZABETH VENTOM. I am in the employ of Frederick Wright Cook, of Oxford-street, a stay-maker. On the 25th of February, about half-past eight o'clock in the evening, I was at work in his shop, and heard a rattle at a pane of glass which had been broken, and over which a paper was placed - I heard the cry of Stop thief! I then missed a pair of stays from the window - I went to the watch-house, and the prisoner was there - the stays were brought in soon afterwards - there were two pieces of paper at the window pinned together - and one of them had been pushed in.(Property produced and sworn to.)
THOMAS ROBERTS. About half-past eight o'clock I was with Bertraun, in Oxford-street, and saw the prisoner with another person; the prisoner took the stays from the window, and gave them to his companion - they ran across Woodstock-street - Bertraun followed them, crying Stop thief? the prisoner threw down the stays, and I saw him stopped by a watchman. I went with Bertraun to a doctor's to take a mad-dog - I then went to the watch-house, and saw the prisoner - I am certain he was the person.
ANGELIOUS BERTRAUN. I was with Foster, and saw the prisoner, and another lad, at the window; I did not see the stays taken, but I saw the other put them under his coat - they crossed over, and I ran after them, towards Shepherd-street - I saw the man throw the stays down.
JOSEPH CASLADE. I heard the cry of Stop thief! and saw the prisoner run across Oxford-street, and down Chapel-place, where I took him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was running down the street, - the gentleman stopped me, and the watchman came and took me.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18260406-281]
854. WILLIAM ORANGE and JOHN JOHNSON were indicted for stealing, on the 13th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of William Thomas Brough, from his person.
WILLIAM THOMAS BROUGH. I am a tailor, and live in Bartholomew-close. This handkerchief is mine - I lost it between Finsbury-square and Whitecross-street - I had seen it ten minutes before.
WILLIAM BOLTON. I am a constable. I was going into Whitecross-street, and saw the two prisoners following four gentlemen - I saw Orange soon after come round a waggon, with this handkerchief in his hand - I caught hold of him, and took up the handkerchief, which he threw under the waggon - I could not see that Mr. Brough was one of the gentlemen they were following.
JOHN FOWELL. I was in Whitecross-street, and saw them - I saw Orange take the handkerchief out of Mr. Brough's pocket, and put it into his bosom half way - he ran into the road; Bolton took him, and he threw it under the waggon; I did not see Johnson do any thing.
ORANGE'S Defence. I came up to a waggon, and I ran to get out of the way.
JOHNSON'S Defence. I was running by a coach, and a waggon was coming in - the officer took hold of me, and said I had been with Orange, but I had not seen him do any thing.
ORANGE - GUILTY.
Whipped and Discharged.
JOHNSON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-282]
855. ESAU SPENCER and CATHERINE WOOD SPENCER were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of February, 1 watch, value 2l.; 4 seals, value 1l., and 1watch-key, value 2s., the goods of William Bleading, from his person.
The prosecutor did not appear. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-283]
856. JOHN ERBY was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of March, 8 bushels of oats, chaff, and beans, mixed, value 30s., the goods of William Waterhouse and Joseph Waterhouse, his masters.
JOSEPH WATERHOUSE. I am in partnership with my father, William Waterhouse - we keep the Swan with Two Necks, Lad-lane. The prisoner was driver of one of our corn vans - he was sent on the 8th of March with this property. I have a list of where he was to leave it.
JAMES CONSTABLE. I was employed by Messrs. Waterhouse, at Brook-street, near Brentford. On the 8th of March the prisoner left me four sacks of mixed corn - he should have left me five; I told him there was one short; he said his master had no more corn in the yard, and he would bring six on the Wednesday afterwards, but on that day he brought but four; I told him I should send his master word of it - he said I might send and be d-d; he appeared to be drunk on the 15th, but on the 8th he said it was all right enough about the corn.
JOHN FIELDING. I am horse-keeper to Messrs. Waterhouse, at Brentford. The prisoner should have brought me four sacks on the 8th of March, and he brought but three.
JOHN PROSSER. I am landlord of the Harry public-house, Mile-end. The prisoner called at my house several times.
SAMUEL JOHNSON. I am granary-man in the service of Messrs. Waterhouse. I gave the prisoner twenty-seven sacks on the 8th of March - he was to take eighteen to Roam's stable, near Romford, five to Brook-street, and four to Brentwood.
WILLIAM MORGAN. I am an officer. I took up the prisoner, and as he was going along Holborn he said he was afraid he should be sent to Newgate, for William Middleton had enticed him to do it, and he had but 3s. out of the 12s., and he was a great fool for doing it - that he had sold the two sacks for 6s. a sack.
Prisoner. I was drawn into it by the horse-keeper at the Harry; he asked me a great many times before I would do it, and said he would let me in at the back gate.
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor, having had a good character.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18260406-284]
857. WILLIAM SEARLE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of January, 1 bed, value 25s.; 2 pillows, value 3s.; 1 pillow-case, value 1s., and 1 sheet, value 2s., the goods of Mary Poulton, widow, in a lodging-room.
MARY POULTON. I am a widow, and live in Union-buildings, Leather-lane. The prisoner came at the latter end of October, to lodge with me, and left on the 21st of January, without notice - I went into the room, and found a note, with some duplicates of the property stated in the indictment.
FRANCIS RAMSAY. I am a pawnbroker. I have a feather bed and blanket, pawned by a female.
MICHAEL OSBOURNE. I am a pawnbroker. I have some articles pawned by a female.
WILLIAM JORDAN. I apprehended the prisoner about a month ago. I asked if he knew a person of the name of Poulton - he said, "I know what it is about - it is about the bed and other things - it was poverty made me do it, but I have now got a service, and shall soon be able to redeem them" - he had hardly a thing to cover him.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18260406-285]
858. GEORGE PAYNE was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of March, 5 handkerchiefs, value 20s., and 1 shawl, value 20s., the goods of Charles Cleverly and James Hutcheson, his masters.
CHARLES CLEVERLY. I am in partnership with James Hutcheson - we live in Chiswell-street; the prisoner was in our service.
WILLIAM HAYES. I am a pawnbroker. These handkerchiefs were brought to me by the prisoner, on the 11th of March - he said he bought them at Gravesend of Mr. Hurley - I detained him - he made great resistance.
THOMAS GARDNER. I am a constable, and took him in charge. I found the shawl in his coat pocket.(Property produced and sworn to).
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Strongly recommended to Mercy. - Confined Eight Days.
[trialAccount t18260406-286]
859. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 99 yards of Canvass, value 3l. 10s., the goods of Benjamin Gawthorne.
HENRY CLAYTON. I am an apprentice to Benjamin Gawthorne, who lives in Great Portman-street. About a quarter before eight o'clock, last Friday night, I was told a person was walking up and down, and I supposed they were after the canvass - I stopped, and watched the prisoner, who was at the shop, and saw him take this canvass, and walk off with it - I pursued him - a person met him and turned him round - I then took him.
HENRY STOWELL. I took the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. It was laying against a baker's shop - the witness came out - I asked if the property belonged to her - "Yes," says he - "Then take it," says I.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18260406-287]
860. MARIA PEARMAN, ELIZABETH MAYWOOD, and ANN SUMMERS, were indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 1 coat, value 3l.; 1 gown, value 14s., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 6s., the goods of William Hughes.

861. WILLIAM MANNING was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 25 bushels of soot, value 18s., the goods of Robert Wiltshire.
The prosecutor did appear. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-289]
862. WILLIAM KING was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 1 coat, value 15s., the goods of Benjamin Hart.
BENJAMIN HART. I am a slopseller, and live in Rosemary-lane; the prisoner was my shopman. I received information from Greenwood - and accused him of taking a coat - he said he had taken it to the Black Dog public-house, in Denmark-street, to raffle - he asked me, the night before, if he should take it, and I said No, as he was addicted to drinking.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you propose to me to take it on the Monday evening to have it raffled for? A. No; I declined it.
BOYD SILVESTER. I am an officer. I found the coat in the possession of the prisoner's wife.
JOHN GREENWOOD. I am shop-boy to Mr. Hart - I saw the prisoner take the coat - he said he was going to take it, but did not say where.
Prisoner. Q. What were the words I said to you? A. You said "John, I am going to take this coat;" I told the Magistrate you said it was to raffle for.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-290]
863. THOMAS HUSSEY and WILLIAM THOMPSON were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 1 knife-case, value 2s.; 23 knives, value 10s., and 25 forks, value 8s., the goods of Edmund Horsefall.
EDMUND HORSEFALL. I live in Gloucester-place, Camden-town. This knife-case, knives, and forks, are mine; I saw them safe on Saturday morning last, when I opened the window - the servant left the door open while she went for a pail of water - a man came in and took them; I was in the kitchen, and saw him go out with his hat on.
JOHN BOSTON. I am a porter, and live in Henry-street, Hampstead-road. About half-past nine o'clock on the morning of the 8th of April, I saw the two prisoners and two other persons - I watched and saw Thompson go to Mr. Horsefall's, No. 11, Gloucester-place - he had nothing when he went in, but he came out with a mahogany case before him - I raised a cry and he handed it to Hussey, who turned a corner, but they were pursued; I helped to secure Hussey in a house into which he had run - he was making his escape out of the back door.
GEORGE MACHIN. I saw the witness in Camden-town last Saturday, about half-past nine o'clock - he called to Mr. Davis, the tobacconist, next door, and called me - I ran out - I saw Hussey lay down the knife-case, and I took it up - he then turned a corner - I did not see Thompson.
ABRAHAM LORIMER. I am an officer. - I got the case of knives from Machin - I shewed it to Mr. Horsefall, who claimed it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
HUSSEY'S Defence. I was going up the road, and I met two young men, who asked me to carry it for them, and said they would give me 2s. - they went into the house, brought it out, and gave it me; when I heard the cry of Stop thief! I put it down and ran.
THOMPSON'S Defence. I was about half a mile from the place - the man who took me came, and said his house had been robbed, and he did not know but I might be one of them.
HUSSEY - GUILTY. Aged 22.
THOMPSON - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18260406-291]
864. ANN FITZHENRY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of April, 1 half-crown, 13 shillings, and 6 sixpences, the goods of Thomas Ashton, from his person.
THOMAS ASHTON. I was in Holborn last Friday night, about eight o'clock; the prisoner caught hold of me, and asked where I was going - I said home; she put her hand into my waistcoat pocket, and took out my money, which I had seen a quarter of an hour before - I caught hold of her and gave her to the watchman. I was quite sober.
JAMES SULLIVAN. I am a watchman. I heard the cry of Watch! and I went out of my box - a gentleman, who was going away, said he saw the prisoner put her hand into the prosecutor's pocket - I took her to the watch-house, and found on her thirteen shillings, six sixpences, and half-a-crown - she said she had 1l. 11s. 6d., which her husband gave her.
Prisoner's Defence. I had the money in my pocket - it was my own - the watch-house-keeper's wife told the prosecutor not to come in in such an indecent condition.
JAMES SULLIVAN. No; there is not a word of truth in it - the prisoner was rather drunk but knew what she was about.
JURY to THOMAS ASHTON. Q. Do you always carry your money in your waistcoat pocket? A. I had a hole in my trousers pocket - I had a sixpence more in my pocket- she took it all at one time.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18260406-292]
865. BENJAMIN BLOUNT was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of March, 1 plough-plane, value 5s., the goods of Thomas Pope.
THOMAS POPE. I am a carpenter. I was at work with the prisoner at Mr. Irbert's yard, Forum-street, Berkeley-square; I had a plough-plane in a box there, and lost it on the 23d of March; I asked the prisoner if he had seen it - he said he had not.
JOHN LUCAS. I live in Rochester-row, Westminster-bridge. The prisoner lodged with me about thirteen weeks - he brought a plough to my house, about a fortnight before he was taken; he said he brought it because he took one from me and pawned it, and brought that instead - he said he went into Forum-street, Berkeley-square, to borrow it for me - I said it was not so good as mine; I gave it to Hopkins.
JAMES LLOYD HOPKINS. I am an officer. I have the plough.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 45.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18260406-293]
866. GEORGE PARKER and JAMES PARKER were indicted for stealing, on the 6th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 2s. 6d., the goods of James Shilcott, from his person.
JAMES SHILCOTT. I am a servant. I was at the Opera-house door on Thursday, the 6th of April, and saw the two prisoners together, just behind me - I put my hand to my pocket, and missed my handkerchief - they were taken into custody about two minutes afterwards, and I saw the handkerchief taken from George Parker's bosom; I had felt it safe five minutes before.
Cross-examined by Mr. CRESWELL. Q. Were there many other persons about? A. Yes; but the prisoners were talking together.
SAMUEL WELLS. I was at the Opera-house. The prosecutor told me he had lost his handkerchief, and I found it on George Parker.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE PARKER's Defence. My brother had nothing to do with it - I picked it up.
Five witnesses gave the prisoners a good character.
GEORGE PARKER - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined One Month.
JAMES PARKER. - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-294]
867. GEORGE CLARKE, JAMES CLARKE, and THOMAS BRANSCOMB were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 2 live tame ducks, price 5s., the property of John Dixon.
JAMES DIXON. I am a glass-cutter, and live in Park-place, Hare-street-fields. On Saturday last I had two live ducks, which slept under the stairs at night, and they run out in the morning - I saw them safe at half-past six o'clock in the morning; I received information, and about one o'clock in the day I saw the prisoners eating some pastry, and supposed they had sold the ducks, and got the money to buy it; I asked George Clarke about them, and he said he had not seen them - he was a neighbour; I looked into his basket, and saw the dung of the duck - he had seen me before I came up to him, and told James Clarke to run away; James afterwards told me he had sold them to a Mr. Clarke, in Montague-street; I went there and got them - I know them to be mine.
CHARLES BARRY. I am a butcher. I was near the prosecutor's house on Saturday last, about seven o'clock in the morning, and saw four boys pinning the ducks up in a corner against the house - George Clarke then caught one and put it into a basket - one of the others caught the other, but I cannot say which - I told a woman of it.
ISAAC CLARK. I am a poulterer, and live in Old Montague-street. On Saturday morning, about eight o'clock, George Clarke brought the ducks to me, and said they were his own, and that he lived opposite Mr. Wells, the farrier - I gave him half a crown for them; I put them into my yard till the prosecutor came.
ISAIAH BANBURY. I was coming from my own home a little before eight o'clock, and I saw George Clarke with two ducks - and a man, whom the last witness employs at times looked at them, and the witness asked, out at the window, what price they were, and the boy said, "Half a Crown, "and that he lived at No. 5, Charles-street, opposite Mr. Wells, the farrier; I afterwards saw Joseph Clarke, near the door of the Compasses, public-house, with some silver in his hand, and a fourth boy was near him; they went to several places to get change - I saw some halfpence in their hands, and they were gone in a moment.
ROBERT WOOD. I am an officer. I bring the ducks, which I got from Clarke's - he would not give them up till I went to get a summons - then he gave them up.
BRANSCOMB's Defence. I was asleep on Saturday morning, and George came and called me about half-past six o'clock, and I went with him.
GEORGE CLARKE - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Whipped and Discharged.
JOSEPH CLARKE. - NOT GUILTY.
BRANSCOMB. - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-295]
SIXTH DAY. WEDNESDAY, APRIL 12.
London Cases, First Jury.
Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
868. JAMES PAYNE alias JOHN BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of February, 1 wrapper, value 6d., and 20 yards of woollen cloth, value 15l., the goods Zachary Langton and others, his partners.
HENRY BATTEN. I am clerk in the employ of Messers. Pickford and Co. I received a truss from the Angel Inn, Fleet-market, on Thursday, the 23d of February, directed to Joshua Lever, Bolton, Lancashire; we were to convey it to Manchester.
JAMES POWNER. I am clerk to Messrs. Pickford. On the evening of the 23d of February I was in the office, at the Castle Inn, Wood-street, and saw the prisoner come off the shafts of a waggon, with a truss on his shoulder - it was going to the City basin, and was directed to James Lever, Bolton. I followed him, and stopped him in Wood-street; the truss fell and I fell - he ran away; I followed, and called Stop thief! I saw him in a few minutes, and have no doubt of him.
THOMAS DAVIS. I am in the employ of Messrs. Pickford and Co. I loaded a truss on their waggon in the yard on the 23d of February, about half-past ten o'clock; on the fore part of it - it was directed to Joshua Lever, Bolton, Lancashire.
JAMES GREIVES. On this evening I was at my door, opposite the gateway. I heard the cry of Stop thief! ran out, and saw the truss in the street; I took it up - the witness ran after the prisoner, down Love-lane, and in a minute afterwards I saw him brought back.
JAMES HARRISON. I work for Messrs. Pickford. I saw Grieves take the truss up, and brought it to the office while the prisoner was in the yard.
JOHN BARNES. I am a constable. I received the prisoner and this truss.
JAMES POWNER. This is the truss - it is the property of Messrs. Pickford and Co - Zachary Langton is one of their partners.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18260406-296]
869. STEPHEN KEY and JOHN JONES were indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Richard Cleobury, from his person.
RICHARD CLEOBURY. On the evening of the 31st of March, I was in Queen-street, Cheapside - I had a yellow silk handkerchief in my pocket - I saw the two prisoners with another person - Girton came to me, and asked if I had lost one - the two prisoners went to the right, and the other to the left.
THOMAS GIRTON. I am an officer. I saw the prisoners at the end of Sweeting's-alley - I followed them to Queen's-street, and saw them follow MR. Cleobury; about half-way down the street key picked his pocket of a handkerchief - the other was close to him, and Jones was behind, but close together - I followed them till they crossed the street - I went to Mr. Cleobury and told him - I then followed the prisoners and took them - the third one did not stay with them a minute after it was done -the prisoners had three handkerchiefs; but not Mr. Cleobury's.
Cross-examined. Q. You saw the man pick the pocket and did not touch him? - A. No; he did not go quite one hundred yards.
JONES's Defence. There was a man playing music - I went down the street to hear it, and met Key there - Mr. Cleobury came with the officer, and they took us.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-297]
870. JOHN WATKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 53lbs. of cheese, value 35s., the goods of George Fitch and Thomas Fitch.
GEORGE FITCH. I am in partnership with my brother Thomas, we are cheesemongers, and live in Leadenhall-street - this cheese is ours - it was on a pile near the door.
JAMES DODD. I am in the prosecutor's service. On the 28th of February I missed something from the door; I went out and saw the prisoner with the cheese under his left arm - I called Stop thief! - he dropped it and ran with all speed; I pursued him till the officer stopped him; I had seen this cheese safe about half an hour before.
The prisoner put in a written defences stating that Dodd, at the time, could not identify him, but said he thought him the man.
JAMES DODD re-examined. I am sure he is the man who dropped the cheese; I lost sight of him as he turned round by the five lamps, and was taken in Fenchurch-street.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18260406-298]
871. SUSAN JAMESON was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of March, 2 half-crowns, 6 shillings, and 2 sixpences, the monies of Benjamin Fish, from his person.
BENJAMIN FISH. On the 26th of March I was in Little Moor-fields, between twelve and one o'clock at night, and saw the prisoner; she said "Young man, I want to speak to you, come down this little alley;" we staid there some time, and as soon as she was gone I missed my money from my right hand trousers' pocket; I had unbuttoned my trousers; I did not give her any money - she was taken to the watch-house in about ten minutes - my purse was left in my pocket and two sixpences in it - six shillings were taken from the prisoner's bosom, and a sixpence from her mouth; I had been drinking, but was perfectly aware I had lost my money.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you give me 6s.? A. No; none at all.
JOSEPH HORTON. I am a constable. I found five shillings and two sixpences in the prisoner's breast - I asked her if that was all she had got - she said Yes; but I thought she talked rather thick, and found two half-crowns and one shillings in her mouth - the prosecutor had stated that his money was 12s., in half-crowns, shillings, and sixpences.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18260406-299]
872. ANN WILKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, 1 lace cap, value 6s., the goods of James Edwards.
JAMES EDWARDS. I lost a lace cap on the 14th of March, from my front room on the first floor. I was sitting in the back room and heard a noise; I went into the room - I saw the prisoner - I asked what she did there - my wife came in and missed the cap - I sent for an officer, but before he came she pulled the cap out of her pocket, put it on her head, and said it fitted her very well - she was in liquor.
SARAH EDWARDS. I went into the room, and the prisoner took this cap from her pocket.
GUILTY. Aged 45.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18260406-300]
873. JOHN SAVAGE was indicted for stealing on the 23d of February, 1 cask, value 8s., and 8 gallons of Cloves cordial, value 3l. 16s., the goods of John Gillyatt Booth, and another, his partner.
JOSEPH SMITH. I am servant to Messrs. John Gillyatt Booth, and his partner - they live in Cow-cross-street. On the 23d of February, I was in White-cross-street - I went up a yard, and left my waggon with some casks in it; I came back, and saw a man in the waggon, who gave the prisoner a cask out, and ran away; the prisoner went down Whitecross-street, with the cask on his shoulder - he walked as fast as he could, and I stopped him - he brought the cask back - it contained eight gallons of cloves cordial.
JACOB COHEN. I saw the prisoner with the cask - he said he had to carry it to Smithfield for sixpence.
JOSEPH HORTON. I am a constable. I was coming down Whitecross-street and saw a number of people - the witness had hold of the prisoner by the collar - I took charge of him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was employed to take it to Smithfield.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18260406-301]
874. LYDIA LEGGETT was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of January, 111/4 yards of thread-lace, value 4l. 6s.; 3 yards of blond-lace, value 3s.; 3 yards of ribbon, value 1s. 6d.; 61/4 yards of muslin trimming, value 5s. 2d.; 2 bands of work, value 2s.; 1 garnet, ring, value 1s., and 1 ivory fan, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Elizabeth Dimes, spinster, her mistress.
ELIZABETH DIMES. I am single and keep a show-room in John-street, Bedford-row. The prisoner was my servant, and had access to my property - I had not missed any thing but the ring and the blond-lace.
Cross-examined by Mr. LAW. Q. Where did you live at the time? A. I lived part of the time in Chapel-street; I found part of my property at Mr. Arthur's, in Bird-in-hand-court, Cheapside; the fan and trimming were found in a box which she had bought of me before she left, and some were found under her bed; there are still many things missing, which I have not found.
SUSANNAH DOCKER. I was in the prosecutrix's service. I went with her to the room in Bird-in-hand-court - the ring and blond lace were in a small box under the prisoner's bed.
Cross-examined. Q. Was this at Mrs. Arthur's? A. Yes; the prisoner had no character when she came, but a sister of her's lived with Miss Dimes - she went from there to Mr. Arthur's.
THOMAS DIMES. I was present when the articles werefound - the prisoner was protesting that she had nothing more but what she had given up - these things were found in the room afterwards - when the fan was produced she asked if my sister could swear to it.
REST KNIGHT. I am an officer. I was sent for, and took the prisoner with the property.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Months.
